## Session 239 (2026-06-12) — Backend live; v0.3 sync; critical pass; disclosure scrub

Arc: Anchor's as-built message (`/receive`) → fakes+client sync → contract/CF/handover docs to v0.3 → 3-agent critical pass (contract conformance / disclosure leak hunt / publish-flow robustness) → fix wave → live API probe. Captain deployed Anchor's slice mid-session. 804 vitest + share e2e green; both tools build.

### Synced to as-built (Anchor's two adjustments + extras)
- **PATCH/DELETE are slug-addressed** (`/pages/<slug>/`) — client, fakes, share-room call sites, tests. Publish response unchanged (`{id, slug, url}`; id stays in the mirror for display).
- **Locked error enum**: + `title_too_long` · `intent_invalid` · `forbidden` · `unauthorized`; 401 carries the envelope (dual shape w/ legacy `detail` — live-probed exact). Server validates title ≤ 200 + intent set; fakes encode both.
- **Quota 20/account** (was 10 in v0.2). Empty payload `{}` = valid active page (200, not 410).
- Base path + www host pinning were already right in all three fetchers (CF purge keys on `www.onlyworlds.com`).

### Critical pass findings → fixes (3 reviewer agents, findings verified before acting)
- **BLOCKER (disclosure)**: prose + frozen-circle markdown shipped raw `[label](ow://type/uuid)` for NON-included elements — the public payload JSON leaked type+uuid+linkable identity even though renderers stripped it visually. Fix: **`scrubMarkdownForPayload()`** in `core/share-payload.ts` — build-time pre-resolution (contract §2's reading); included targets keep links, bare hand-typed URIs resolve to names. Render-time rule stays as defense-in-depth. Test-locked (payload JSON asserted uuid-free).
- **XSS**: markdown `[text](javascript:…)` rendered as live links in BOTH renderers (public visitors + arbitrary pasted payloads). Fix: scheme allowlist (`https?://`, `mailto:`, `#`) in `writing-compiled.ts` + `show/render.ts`; unsafe scheme → plain text.
- **Compact cards** now identity-only incl. image (image ships/renders only with a full card — §5.1.1); enforced in builder + all 3 render paths.
- **Delete-while-published** orphaned the server row (quota leak, no recovery): deletePage now always confirms (names the public URL) and **revokes first**; abort on revoke failure; `not_found` = already gone, proceed.
- **Stale-mirror dead ends**: republish→`not_found` clears the mirror (page returns to publishable); revoke→`not_found` marks revoked locally. (Likely to fire after backend DB resets.)
- **Publish-flow hardening**: `await writeChain` before payload build (rename-then-publish shipped stale title); mirror-write failure no longer toasts success (URL preserved in the error); in-flight guards on republish/revoke; `mountedView` guard before rebuild (no cross-view clobber); republish adopts server `updated_at`.
- **Revival escape hatch**: "use a different URL" button in the revoked state (admin-locked slugs were a permanent dead end).
- **Fakes fidelity**: revive-counts-against-quota (VERIFY-LIVE) · admin_locked beats revoked-404 on PATCH (was dead code) · cross-account mutations → 403 `forbidden` (VERIFY-LIVE) · Cache-Control/ETag on the public GET · `slug_invalid` toast surfaces server detail.
- **Viewer hardening**: `parsePayload` validates per-block shape (drop/paste feeds arbitrary JSON); paste/drop errors render inline (landing no longer self-destructs); fetch failures `console.error` for smoke-day diagnosis.

### Live API probe (read-only, 2026-06-12)
404 + 401 envelopes match the fakes exactly · CORS allows `show.onlyworlds.com` AND `localhost:3045` (api-key/api-pin headers, PATCH/DELETE methods) with `Vary: origin` — the cached-ACAO concern is structurally resolved · `Cf-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC` → **CF Cache Rule still to click** (the one shared config piece, `SHARING_CF_DELIVERY.md` §3).

### LIVE SMOKE (Captain — supersedes + includes the 238 checklist)
**Part A — composition + disclosure (from 238, unchanged):**
1. Disclosure flip: full card w/ relation field → preview → related element plain text → card it too → now a chip.
2. Prose disclosure: @-mention something not carded → preview → plain text, no chip.
3. Copy payload → paste somewhere → §5 JSON. **NEW: confirm no `ow://` URIs for non-carded elements anywhere in it.**
4. Local-only world → share room shows "needs an online world", no publish controls.
5. Featured in: card-bound element → detail → "Featured in" chip → share room.
6. Circle bridge: known-view → "create page →" → page with compiled knowledge; preview honest.
7. "+ circle" picker offers collectives only; live compiled preview.

**Part B — live publishing (NEW — the backend is up):**
8. Publish on an api-linked world (messworld): slug prefilled → publish → success toast with `show.onlyworlds.com/<slug>` URL.
9. `curl https://www.onlyworlds.com/api/sharing/pages/<slug>/` → 200 envelope with your payload (viewer isn't deployed yet, so the toast URL 404s at the host — expected until Hangar ships `tools/show`).
10. Edit a block → republish → curl again → new payload.
11. Revoke → curl → 410 `revoked`. Republish (revival) → curl → 200 with content.
12. Slug collision: second page, same slug → "already taken" toast.
13. Rapid-edit safety: knowledge room — type prose, immediately click a held-by add — both land (#79).

**Part C — delivery (when `show` deploys + Cache Rule on):** `SHARING_CF_DELIVERY.md` §5 (cache HIT, purge-on-republish, OG unfurl, CORS items 6-7).

### Session 239, second arc (post-deploy build wave) — smoke additions
14. **Card images**: full card → banner; compact card → small head avatar; per-card "Show image" toggle in the card editor (off on every card = image stays out of the payload).
15. **Element images in atlas**: Stuffington's image renders in element detail (banner, hover × hides it) + context-pane thumb; header type icon becomes a clickable thumbnail while hidden (click = re-show). Setting: Settings → Editing → "Show element images".
16. **Theme (S3)**: share page detail → Theme section → flip accent/font/mode/density/width, set a cover URL → preview reflects it; copy payload → `theme` carries ONLY valid tokens; republish → live page restyles.
17. **Drag-reorder**: drag a block row above/below another (cyan insert line); arrows still work.
18. **Collection block**: "+ collection" → pick elements → identity grid with avatars; preview + payload identity-only (no fields leak).
19. **Open-from-block**: hover a card/circle block → `open_in_new` control jumps to the element.
20. **List badges + reconcile**: share list shows live/revoked badges; entering the list on an api-linked world quietly heals publish state from the server (toast only when something changed).
21. **Known-view rendering**: knowledge known-view quotes now render prose with chips — no more raw `[Name](ow://…)` markdown.
22. **Map block (S7)**: "+ map" → pick a map with a web image → pins checklist (all on) → preview: image with pin dots. A local-media-only map shows the honest "needs a web image URL" hint. Anchor's reply also landed: fakes flipped to her confirmed behaviors (cross-account 404, PATCH-revive 200, quota 429, reserved slugs 409).
23. **Map labels + pin modal** (third wave): pin labels show by default ("Show pin labels" toggle in the map editor → hover-only); **clicking a pin opens a small element modal** (image, name, type, description, populated field rows) — works in preview AND the viewer. NOTE the disclosure shift: included pins now ship their element's full populated fields (the checklist is the gate; hint on "Visible pins" says so).
24. **Republish freshness**: republish → reload the viewer → fresh content immediately (the browser-cache fix — show fetches with `no-cache`, revalidating via ETag). REQUIRES the show v0.2 push/redeploy. Suggested to Anchor: `max-age=0, s-maxage=3600` header split (message sent).

**UI-pass prep**: `Notes/2026-06-12-knowledge-sharing-ui-audit.md` — 26 quick wins + 19 design calls across knowledge/share/show (3-agent audit). The UI runthrough session starts there.

## PRIOR (2026-10-15, SESSION 411: **1.43.1 LIVE** (Boss h11 #5190: deployment `a8c30566`, `main-B-dpMzgV.js`, byte-identical; his control on my mock keel ported to the live build: one toast on 1.43.1, two on 1.43.0, distinct nodes; not seen: a real keel, updates with creates) (Refresh says it once, the journey scout's gap C; gates: typecheck 0 + **2861 vitest, exit 0** (`--no-cache`, 2 skipped) + **468 e2e, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, 467 passed, 1 flaky** `tour-keep:230` (3 of 3 alone, retries off; the same load-sensitive file) + a production build, `main-B-dpMzgV.js`); **1.43.0 LIVE** (Boss h11 #5168: deployment `677172b8`, `main-CRxqyCIF.js`, byte-identical; his control with CDP touches on a Pixel 7 profile at 412: a pinch keeps visualViewport.scale 1 and narrows `data-window` from -21..31 to -1.99..10.01, a one-finger slide moves the start and keeps the span; on 1.42.0 the page zooms to 4.47; not seen by him: a tapped dot's slide, the now-line, a real phone, Firefox slides) (the chart by finger, #301's last part, #301 done; gates: typecheck 0 + **2861 vitest, exit 0** (`--no-cache`, 2 skipped) + **467 e2e, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, 466 passed, 1 flaky** `tour-keep:272` (3 of 3 alone, retries off; the file S409 measured as load-sensitive on both builds; the chart is not in it) + a production build, `main-CRxqyCIF.js`); **1.42.0 LIVE** (Boss h11 #5140: deployment `390bf3c9`, `main-CNv2q9af.js`, byte-identical; his control on a Pixel 7 profile against 1.41.0: Hide types under the tiles, Done, a tap keeps characters out and a second brings them back; none with a mouse; not seen: a real phone, iOS, whether hidden types survive a reload); **1.41.0 LIVE** (Boss h11, after #5099); S410's block is the newest entry in `docs-archive/PROGRESS-history.md`)

- **1.43.0, the chart by finger** (#301 done, its last part): pointer events with `pointerType === 'touch'` drive the chart's own state machine (`handleTouchDown`/`Move`/`Up` in `src/visualization/timeline/timeline-view.ts`; the mouse listeners unchanged). One finger: pan, the strip's band and edges (grip 14px), the now-line (16px), a range's end cap. **A finger re-dates only a dot it has already tapped**, so a slide across a crowded phone chart pans. Two fingers pinch around the date under their midpoint (`zoomWindow`) and pan with it; a second finger stops the first's gesture and writes nothing (a now-line drag goes back). The canvas takes `touch-action: pan-y` (the page no longer zooms under a pinch: visual scale 4.1 before, 1 after). The touch `pointerdown` is cancelled: a tap otherwise also arrives as made-up mouse events and ran the click progression twice, and the peek did not hold (measured in both engines). The rail's drag-to-date stays a mouse's; on touch an undated element is dated on its page. Help's three chart lines have touch versions (tap, tap again to focus; slide a tapped dot, a range by its end, the now-line; pinch and slide sideways). DESIGN_CHARTS (the viewport model), conv. 141 grown, the changelog `touch-chart`. The canvas writes its window to `data-window`.
- **1.43.1, Refresh says it once**: a refresh that brought changes toasted the count twice, once from `pullNowAndRefresh` (`src/sync/sync-attach.ts`) and again from its caller in other words (Settings, This world's Refresh; the worlds list's refresh). The callers now say only "Already up to date"; the pull says the counts, the conflict toasts the rest. Guard: `tests/e2e/refresh-toast.spec.ts` (a linked world on a small keel, one upsert on the feed; red with the old Settings line). My first counter lied: a MutationObserver counting `addedNodes` saw the one toast twice (the manager moves a toast it drew), so it counts distinct nodes; the worlds list's refresh is fixed the same way but not walked.
- **A guard cut**: a quiet window on the mouse handlers after a touch, written as a second layer, stayed green with `preventDefault` gone and with both gone; a probe (`compat-probe.mjs`, `compat-probe-ff.mjs`, known positive on a plain div) showed both engines honour the cancelled pointerdown, so it went, and the spec asserts the mechanism instead (no mousedown reaches the canvas on a tap).
- **Guards**: `tests/e2e/chart-touch.spec.ts`, real touches through CDP in Chromium at 412 with `isMobile`; the tap test also in Firefox (Playwright cannot slide a finger there; named gap). Mutations, each red: no touch listeners (3 of 3), no `touch-action` (3), any dot grips (1), a cursor-wide now grip (1), no `preventDefault` (the tap in both engines). The stretch test's first fixture (a 5..8 range) put the end inside the start glyph's grip at 412, so the slide re-dated the start and 15 past the end was refused: the fixture, not the code; 5..14 now.
- **Walked**: `tools/atlas/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-15-s411/look-chart.mjs` (Pixel 7 Chromium with CDP touches; Firefox taps): the baseline (a swipe did nothing, a pinch zoomed the page), then a 4.2x pinch, a 148px pan that moved 20.8 of a 52 span, a vertical swipe that leaves the chart (nothing at 412 scrolls: the room fits), a tapped card docked under the chart in both engines, no page errors. Frames `Forge/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-15-s411/chart-*`. The waypoint toast covers the card's Edit for its seconds (the toast layer, as in S410).

**Records**: `Forge/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-15-s411/atlas/` (e2e and build logs).

- **1.41.0, dates to check** (#281, Kael's card `date-continuity-checker.md`): Home's loose threads lead with the dates worth a second look, one sentence each, both dates in the world's calendar, both names opening their element. `findDateConflicts` (`src/core/date-continuity.ts`, pure): two kinds cannot be true (a span ending before it starts; an Event's `triggers`, its causes, beginning after it) and three are links across a birth (an Event's or ended Relation's `characters`/`creatures` born once it was over; a Title's `holders` born at or after its revoke), which the standard's "relevant to" and "hold or represent" make possible, so the band states dates and never judges. A bench of 300 small OPFS files (`Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-15-s407/opfs-bench.mjs`): Playwright's Firefox pays about 40 ms a file whichever way, one at a time 13.1 s, all at once 12.3 s, a worker's sync access handles 11.9 s; Chromium 0.75 s, 0.17 s, 0.61 s. So no write path cures Firefox here, and the fix is honest progress: `writeAll` writes `WRITE_CONCURRENCY` (8) at once with each folder looked up once (Chromium's open 1.2 s to 0.3 s; Firefox 16.1 to 13.8 s), world.json still last, a refusal stops the rest, and `onWritten` counts every file into the demo overlay ("Opening Moppetopia… 120 of 311 files"). - **#202, a fixture time bomb, not a flake and not load** (fixed after 1.31.0's push, test-only): `server-delete-bulk.spec.ts`'s mock keel stamped `updated_at` with a FIXED date, `2026-10-11T09:00:xx` UTC, while the browser stamps local edits with the real clock; the engine calls a copy edited when `local_updated_at` is past `server_updated_at`, so from 09:00 UTC on 2026-10-11 every copy read as edited. Every green run of it was before 09:00 UTC and every red one after, at master too. A probe printed both sources under load: the live folder's notice was null, the engine's changed set held all four. I had called it load-triggered (the 6-worker reproducer only coincided with the clock): a known trap that fit the evidence. Fix: the mock stamps with the real clock, monotonic; 6 of 6 under load, then the full suite: 442 (= `--list`), PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, 2 flaky (`tour-keep:103`, `tour-narrow:97`). Observation for SYNC_NOTES, not a fix: that comparison spans two clocks, so a device clock ahead of keel's by D re-pushes an edited element until D has passed (self-healing, since each push moves the server stamp forward). `route2-merge.spec.ts` has the same fixed-clock shape with a past date; its assertions hold today.

- **1.24.1 BUILT** (#141): Admiral Fluffington's web on Moppetopia piled into one knot. Two causes: the sim had no step limit, so two nodes passing close flung a third far out (Medal of Damp Valor at x -986 against a median radius of 174) and the fit to the pane shrank the rest into the middle; and labels had only a count budget (14 by degree), no placement. Now: a step cap (`maxStep`), collision 64 apart (was 56), a slot under the centre kept clear for the subject's label; the label pass `src/tabs/right/graph-labels.ts` (pure): first clear side of eight or hover, the subject and selection always print. Conv. 134; DESIGN_SHELL "The graph".
- **Measured by ink** (the walk, Moppetopia's demo copy, Fluffington): at 1440, depth 1, HEAD drew 51 label-over-label, 139 label-over-node, 307 overlapping node pairs; 1.24.1 draws 0, 0, 0 in Chromium and Firefox, and 0 at 1000 too. 19 of 31 labels print at depth 1, the rest on hover. **Left as is**: at 1200 the pane is 342 wide and the subject's label grazes up to 3 nodes at depth 2, one node pair touching by a pixel; the slot is an estimate at scale 0.75.
- **Guards**: `tests/unit/graph-labels.test.ts`, `tests/unit/context-pane-graph-knot.test.ts` on `tests/fixtures/moppetopia-fluffington-web.json` (his two hops, names and links only). Revert-checked red: the step cap (the outlier test, both depths), the label slot (the slot test, and depth 2's spacing), the label pass's node test (three rows).
- **Live**: the same ink walk on atlas.onlyworlds.com (`walk-live-web.mjs`), Chromium and Firefox, 1440, depths 1 and 2: 0, 0, 0; #141 done.

- **1.25.1 BUILT** (#126): measured first (`probe-demo-time.mjs`, every OPFS call timed, the dev server): the demo open made 627 OPFS writes, half into a scratch copy that is only zipped; Firefox spends about 47 ms per write whatever the concurrency (`probe-opfs-parallel.mjs`: 300 writes, 14.3 s serial, 13.5 s sixteen-wide; Chromium 0.6 s and 0.2 s), so the only lever is fewer writes. The scratch is now an in-memory directory (`src/store/memory-dir.ts`) under the same FsFolderStore: 315 writes, Firefox 55 s to 17.5 s and Chromium 6.4 s to 2.6 s on the dev server. The old OPFS `atlas-scratch/` is swept once. Guards: `tests/integration/memory-dir.test.ts` (the real store on it, a world file read back whole, the browser error names), `try-demo.spec.ts` now asserts no `atlas-scratch` or `try-*` folder is made (red at the old code). **Left**: the world's own 315 writes are about 11 s in Firefox; a worker writing through sync access handles is the next lever, not built.
- **1.25.1 live, timed** (`probe-demo-time.mjs` against atlas.onlyworlds.com, a fresh profile each): Firefox 35.3 s on 1.25.0 to 16.4 and 16.9 s on 1.25.1, Chromium 4.2 s to 1.8 and 2.5 s. About half, not the third the dev server showed (55 s to 17.5 s there); the 1.25.1 changelog said a third, 1.26 corrects it. #126 done.
- **1.26 BUILT** (Remove picture; Boss's removal contract h37 #2354, Keel #170 live, Skeld h20 #2496): an element whose image URL is a picture uploaded into this world offers Remove picture; the question first, counted from this copy (keel holds a second removal ticket for ten minutes), then keel's `remove-ticket` `{key}` (one retry on 404 or `upload_pending`), the edge's `/v1/remove`, and every image URL here that showed it cleared through the normal save. Design truth: DESIGN_SYNC "Removing a picture". Guards: `tests/integration/media-remove.test.ts` (12; the retry revert-checked red), `tests/e2e/picture-upload.spec.ts` (the remove test: a no asks nothing, a yes removes and clears; red without the button). **Not walked live**: the joint test of all three halves on live (upload a throwaway to the Manifest, remove it, the link 404s), owed right after the deploy, before anything is said to users.
- **The joint live test of 1.26** (Boss's go, h11 #2527; `walk-live-remove.mjs`, the Manifest's The Forge, my bay's element): upload, ticket, edge 201, PATCH 200; Remove picture: remove-ticket 200, `/v1/remove` 200, the toast, the image URL cleared in Atlas. **But the clear never reached keel**: no push followed, the pill said Synced. Cause, by reading and measuring: "unsynced" compares this device's stamp with keel's, and keel answers about 1.5 s ahead of this laptop (three Date headers), so an edit within that window after a sync read clean. Two removed throwaways (`…01a12586…png`, `…01a12587…png`) left their dead URL on The Forge on keel. Fixed in 1.26.1 (conv. 135, both stores, guarded, revert-checked red).
- **The joint test, round 2, passed on 1.26.1**: upload, remove, and the clear pushed 4.8 s after the save (PATCH 200); keel's image URL on The Forge is empty again. Boss saw all removed keys gone from the bucket (h11 #2538), and the exact public URLs still answering 200 from the edge cache: removal frees OnlyWorlds and the storage count at once, not the open web (days or longer without the purge token the Captain shelved). The question promised "its link stops working": 1.26.2 says what is true.

## PRIOR (2026-10-10, SESSION 386: **1.24.0 PUSHED** (#140, Fully forget; gates: typecheck 0 + **2693 vitest** (284 files) + **431 e2e (= `--list`, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0; its 1 flaky was the new Firefox forget test reading the fresh boot's storage probe mid-flight, fixed to wait, 10 of 10 after)** + a build); **1.23.0 LIVE** (Boss af8c3b91; #154, Upload picture; walked live: Hyperion linked by its write key shows the row, no write calls, no page errors); **1.22.1 LIVE** (Boss e578da16; #89, the background rename reads the disk; gates on its tree: typecheck 0 + **2680 vitest** + **427 e2e, zero flaky** + a build); **1.22.0 LIVE** (`0f2bc50`, Boss 8616d77c; walked live in Chromium and Firefox: a junk join code stripped, one preview with no referrer, keel's 404, the refusal line, no page errors) (agent seats and the `#/join/` door, OW Infra #104's Atlas half; built by S385, which closed without a handover; gated and fixed by S386). Gates on the 1.22 tree: typecheck 0 + **2678 vitest** (281 files, 2 skipped, exit 0) + **427 e2e (= `--list`, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, 1 flaky: `tour-keep.spec.ts:103`, 5 of 5 alone with retries off; untouched by this change)** + a production build)

**Records**: design `Forge/Notes/2026-10-10-s385-join-door-design.md`; contract `Forge/Notes/2026-10-08-s379-handoff-contract.md` (R1-R9); the room lines h37 #2285 (the ask of keel), #2298.

- **1.22 BUILT**: a world can sit on an agent seat (its own `ow_w_` key and `ow_s_` secret). `src/core/seat.ts` `worldPin` is the one PIN reader for every call made for a world (conv. 132); the secret lives on the registry entry only, never in `world.json`. keel's member refusals (`not_author`, `owner_only`) are per-item failures, not an auth bail (conv. 22 grown). The door: `#/join/<code>` leaves the address bar first (`core/join-capture.ts`), previews, asks (`ui/join-world-modal.ts`), redeems as "Atlas (Firefox)" and the like, downloads (`setup/join-door.ts`). Design truth: DESIGN_SYNC "Seat worlds and the `#/join/` door".
- **The S386 gate** (two opus lenses, credentials and flow; they converged on the reconnect): fixed, each revert-checked red: (1) the reconnect took over ANY linked world with no PIN here, turning an owner's own world into a contributor seat for good, and a view-only world into a seat; now only a seat world that lost its secret adopts a new seat; (2) a world-file restore wrote the registry's `api` block, secret included, straight to `world.json` (`diskApiBlock` now). Fixed, guarded by test: the account PIN prompt took a seat secret and saved it as the account PIN (it now names and refuses one); keel's role is `co_builder`, not `co-builder` (roles in `seatRoleWords`; both fakes now use keel's values and fail closed like `_check_author`). Fixed, by reading: Make online's PIN fields cut a pasted secret at 8 characters (64 now); a whole `/bulk` failure lost keel's status and code (`failureOf` now); sharing's `owner_only` read as an auth failure (its own sentence now); a failed activation after the door said "Atlas opened it" (it checks now); a double Open could redeem twice (one submit at a time); the spare-seat lines named only Members, which a non-owner cannot open (they name "Your agents" too).
- **Own eyes**: Chromium and Firefox, the confirm screen at 1400 and 390 wide, Open, Settings' seat line, no page errors (`Forge/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-10-s386/`, script `tools/atlas/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-10-s386/walk.mjs`).
- **Unannounced on purpose**: the door ships with no changelog entry, because no page makes its link until keel's Open in Atlas button is live (Skeld's next keel piece, h37 #2286). Its entry lands with that button; the comment in `changelog.data.ts` says so.
- **Left as is (the gate's risks, named)**: a redeem whose reply is lost (keel committed, the network dropped it) makes a seat Atlas never hears about, and the retry shows the "someone else used it" line; a failed download then Try again can leave a half-made folder behind (older than the door); a code stays in the browser's global history until it expires (the cure is the 10-minute pass asked of keel's button, not built yet); R4's one seat per browser cannot hold until keel's preview names the world id (Q10); no migration for a seat secret saved as the account PIN by 1.17 to 1.21's link dialog (seats were days old on keel; none known outside the crew).
- **1.22.1** (#89): the background filename tidy wrote the scan's cached body under the canonical name and removed the old file, so an outside edit landing between scan and tidy was lost, and a file with git conflict markers was replaced. It now reads through `readForWrite`, only for a file it renames (reading every file broke four live-folder tests and the deferred open's cost). Guard `tests/integration/folder-store.tidyReadsDisk.test.ts`, red at the old code (conv. 92). Changelog 1.22.1.
- **Not walked live**: a successful join against real keel. The live check: a pass minted on Moppetopia's Access pane, Character unchecked, opened at `atlas.onlyworlds.com/#/join/<code>` (Skeld's #1956 check too).
- **1.23 BUILT** (#154, Keel #59's last leg): an online, writable world's element has an Upload picture row under its image URL; a keel ticket (the world's own PIN), the bytes to the edge with only the ticket, the URL saved through `persistFieldPatch`. Design truth: DESIGN_SYNC "Pictures". Decided (brief `Forge/Notes/2026-10-10-s386-upload-button-brief.md`): Atlas's own adapter, not the SDK client; no re-encode; no X-Key; the public-link sentence in the row. SDK pin 4.7.2 (Boss h11 #2306). Revert-checked: the row's online-only guard (e2e), `worldPin` in the upload, the edge-origin check. Own eyes: `Forge/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-10-s386/picture-uploaded.png` (mocked keel and edge).
- **Not walked live**: a real upload. It leaves a permanent public picture on the account's media host (nothing there can be deleted), so it waits on the Captain naming a world for it, or his own try (h20).
- **1.24 BUILT** (#140): Settings, Storage, "Forget everything in this browser". Design truth and order: conv. 133 (`src/setup/forget-browser.ts`). Gated by one opus lens on the destructive path: it upheld that no step touches a folder on the drive, and found what is now fixed: an online browser world was called "kept on onlyworlds.com" though changes not yet synced are lost (the line says so now); a failure after leaving the open world left a page with no store (it offers only Reload now); no quiet-down before the removal (parked stores evicted, a busy entry retried, the worlds' home removed last); a 1.16 tour world counted as a folder on the drive; seat secrets unnamed; the roll call ran outside the activation chain. Revert-checked: the roll call (e2e), the self-filters (both together), the order and the home-last rule (vitest).
- **Left as is (the forget gate's risks)**: a tab restored from the back/forward cache that missed the `forgotten` message keeps its old registry in memory; a frozen tab's due timer could save once before its reload; the account token is dropped here, not revoked on the server; "folders on disk byte-for-byte unchanged" is by construction (the only `removeEntry` is on the OPFS root) plus the normal save of the open world, not measured on a real drive folder (the e2e's stand-in folder is in OPFS).
- **Not walked live**: the Sikelia view-only case of the picture row (my live script stuck in the view dialog; the guard is code and the local-world e2e); the forget on live.
- ⚡ **NEXT**: Boss deploys 1.24.0, walk Fully forget live once (a fresh profile, one browser world, forget, the landing); the real upload on the Captain's word (h20 #2316); then #141, #139, #126; the live join check when a pass exists. Keel's side of #104: Skeld (the button, Q10, Q5, 5b).

## PRIOR (2026-10-09, SESSION 384: **1.21.0 LIVE** (`85123b8`, world units, #149; Boss f4cf890f, walked live by 23:33 in Chromium and Firefox from fresh contexts, no page errors; #149 closed); 1.20.1 before it. **Records**: the room lines h37 #2250 (what 1.21 does, what it leaves out) and h11 #2249 (Boss). The previous front door, `Forge/Notes/2026-10-09-s383-handover.md`, still holds the queue below it.

- **1.21 BUILT** (the Atlas piece of OW Infra #146, standard 00.31.00, keel 8d6f231, folder spec v0.3.7): `src/core/world-units.ts` maps the nine fields to `length_unit` / `mass_unit` / `distance_unit` (creature speed prints "/round"); `element-detail-fields.ts` sets `data-unit` and CSS prints it after the number (the text stays the bare number; the modal editor label gains "(cm)"); Settings, World tab has Length, Mass and Distance on one row (`settings-world.ts`). Synced as world meta: a trimmed `label` kind in `world-meta-sync.ts`, because keel trims and an untrimmed hand edit would re-PATCH every round. Import (`reconcile.ts`) and export carry them; `FOLDER_FORMAT_VERSION` is 0.3.7. Both fakes trim and cap at 64 as keel does. No SDK repin needed (Atlas calls `/world` directly).
- **Revert-checked**: the trimmed kind (the integration guard goes red with three pushes of " cm "), the `data-unit` line (the e2e goes red).
- **Own eyes**: Chromium and Firefox, the Settings row at two widths, a character and a creature (`Forge/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-09-s384/`, script `tools/atlas/Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-10-09-s384/walk.mjs`). Firefox renders the unit; its `getComputedStyle` does not resolve `attr()` in `content`, so the e2e asserting the computed value is Chromium-only by the project setup.
- **Left out on purpose**: share pages print bare numbers (the payload carries no world meta; a contract change with `tools/show`, its own item if wanted, h37 #2250). No fleet gate: a small revert-checked change on an existing, gated sync path.

## PRIOR (2026-09-28 — SESSION 359: **THE FOLDER AS A SHARED SURFACE, THE FLOOR** — a toolkit review for Skeld found that atlas loses or ignores edits whenever another tool writes the folder; two fixes shipped, the real bout designed and measured; gates: typecheck 0 + **1648 vitest** + **308 e2e (count = `--list` = 308, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, zero flaky)** + build clean; PUSHED `8bbd2f5`, Boss briefed for the 1.6 redeploy)

**Where it came from**: Captain asked for a fine-grain review of the OnlyWorlds toolkit (`Forge/Notes/2026-09-28-toolkit-review.md`). Answering Skeld's three atlas questions from source (not the capability card) turned up: a git branch switch under a linked folder is invisible to atlas; "close atlas" must mean close the TAB (a parked world keeps its cache and a 1970 lock heartbeat); and **an in-place edit to a linked folder never syncs** (push is decided by the in-file stamps). Skeld put all of it into toolkit 4.0 and ruled born-bare. Reply: `.temper/messages/inbox/from-skeld-2026-09-28-born-bare-and-an-atlas-bout.md`.

- **BORN BARE** (`folder-store-fs.ts`, the spelling vote): a NEW pin/marker file writes `map`/`zone` unless some file of its type already uses `map_id`/`zone_id`. Fresh folders are born in the format's spelling; old atlas folders keep theirs. Cost accepted by Skeld: pre-1.3 atlas reads a bare pin as mapless. 2 e2e (`folder-store-fs.spec.ts`: born bare; a suffixed folder stays suffixed), the first revert-checked (map read `null`).
- **AN OUTSIDE EDIT SURVIVES ATLAS'S NEXT WRITE** (`readForWrite`, DESIGN convention 92): update, updateWith and both sync stamps merge into the file as it is on disk, not the cached body. Before this, any atlas save to element X wrote its stale cached copy over another tool's edit to X. One e2e, revert-checked ("as atlas wrote it" came back). **Scope**: keeps outside edits alive; does NOT show them before reopen or make them sync.
- **The bout, designed and measured, not built**: `Forge/Notes/2026-09-28-shared-surface-design.md`. Sync manifest in `.atlas/` (server stamp + content hash per element) replacing the in-file stamps; an "N elements changed outside Atlas" prompt; background re-read on revive; a clean-copy export; "copy as AI context" parked for Captain. **Numbers** (Sikelia, 11,428 files, real Chromium): SHA-256 of every file ~25 ms (known-answer checked against node) · OPFS stat pass 1.3-1.8 s vs full read 3.8-5.3 s. Probes: `scripts/probe-hash-cost.mjs`, `scripts/probe-revive-cost.mjs`. The manifest is a persisted cross-version contract: **gate it before building.**
- **Changelog 1.6** (two entries, `folders` tag) regenerated. Capability card §2b "Sharing the folder with other tools" added and stale lines trued (Firefox gets the tour only, export envelope shipped, the now reads both spellings, no copy-world feature). io docs `docs/tools/atlas.md` trued to 1.5 (uncommitted in `onlyworlds.github.io`).
- ⚡ **SMOKE (Captain, next atlas session, ~5 min)**:
  1. Create a new world, place a pin on a map, open the pin's JSON file in the folder: it says `"map"`, not `"map_id"`.
  2. Open an OLD world that already has pins, add a pin: the new file matches its siblings (`map_id`).
  3. With a world open in atlas, edit an element's description in its JSON file with a text editor. In atlas, change a DIFFERENT field of that element (e.g. motivations). The file keeps both edits, and atlas now shows the new description on that element too (its save re-read the file). Another element edited outside atlas still shows its old text until you reload the tab; that is the next bout.
  4. Changelog page shows 1.6 with its two entries.

## PRIOR (2026-09-11 — SESSION 356: **THE DENSITY LAYER** — Sikelia at 4,771 elements is the first world too big for the map, and the map grew up; gates after bout three: typecheck 0 + **1648 vitest** + **305 e2e (count = `--list` = 305, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, zero flaky, quiet machine)** + build clean; three commits pushed this session: `3d8736c` density, `c06acf7` zone panel + world styles, bout three after)

**The fixture**: Skeld's `sikelia-world` (commit `1da9dd1`: 196 pins, 332 zones, 3,143 markers) opened whole from a byte-seeded OPFS copy, 4,771/4,771 element files, zero warnings. Captain's screenshot of the same world from the wire: a wall of labels, and a route drawn as a filled polygon. Shots: `Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-09-11-s356/`.

- **LINE ZONES FROM OTHER TOOLS DREW AS POLYGONS, AND IT WAS ATLAS.** Skeld's writer stores shape in `supertype: "Line"`; `getZoneShape`'s legacy fallback compared case-sensitively against zoner's lowercase `line`. Case-folded now (3 unit tests, revert-checked). Reported to Skeld with the convention question (only atlas writes `atlas_shape`; zoner reads lowercase; a schema field is the real fix).
- **THE DENSITY LAYER** (`tabs/right/map-label-density.ts`, DESIGN_MAPS §11): `pin_labels`/`zone_labels` gain `'auto'` ("Fit"), the new factory default. Greedy collision pruning in priority order (selected > pins by world degree > polygons > lines, bigger first), four placements per pin label (below/right/left/above), pin icons are obstacles a label never covers except that the top tenth of pins by weight may sit over a LIGHTER icon as a last resort (Syrakousai ringed by its harbour towns). Boxes are COMPUTED from projected anchors + canvas text measure with one calibration rect per pass: the first version read 700 rects and cost 20-90ms a zoom, this one 8-30ms with a 1ms kernel. Culling is a class (`atlas-label-culled`), never `display`. **The icon-scale rule**: an icon is never wider than ~70% of the median nearest-neighbour gap at the current zoom (17px at Sikelia's fit, 32px one zoom in), patched in place on the `<img>` rather than `setIcon`. Sparse maps are byte-identical to before. Measured on rendered ink: 87 labels at fit, 0 overlaps. 15 kernel unit tests + a 40-pin-cluster e2e, revert-checked.
- **HOVER LIGHTS THE WHOLE ZONE** (Captain's ask): selected weight, full stroke, +0.15 fill, a lighter glow, and the label revealed even when culled (`atlas-label-revealed` beats `atlas-label-culled`).
- **Toolbar order**: settings | show zones · show pins · fit | draw polygon · draw line (looking before making). **Copy**: the non-Chromium landing note opens "Atlas requires…", the tour sentence and the "one sentence becomes a small linked world" tagline are gone (Captain).
- **Changelog 1.5** (two entries) regenerated. DESIGN_ZONES §1.5 carries the other-writers note.
- **★ BOUT TWO — THE ZONE PANEL + WORLD STYLES (same day; Captain: go, subtype too, keep the fold-out's feel).** `core/world-styles.ts` (pure: rules keyed on type + supertype/subtype, case-folded; `resolveStyle` = own `atlas_*` > subtype rule > supertype rule; `groupByKind`; the taxonomy hints) + `core/world-styles-state.ts` (the `styles` slice, loaded at activation after the elements, saved through `FolderStore.readStyles/writeStyles` to **`.atlas/styles.json`**, beside charts/tombstones/sync-state). `tabs/right/map-zone-panel.ts`: the fold-out is now kinds as collapsible groups (swatch = the rule's colour picker, eye = hide the kind on this map via `entry.hiddenZoneKinds`, tune = fill / weight / dash / "label first" / write-into-zones / clear, caret → chips), a search box, hover-a-row lights the zone (`ZoneOverlay.setZoneHovered`), click selects + pans. `ZoneShape` gained `weight`/`dash`; the zone label's hairline takes the zone colour. The density layer ranks `label_priority` kinds with the pins. Taxonomy swatches (`styleValueHint`/`styleRowHint`) beside the field label in element detail and on sidebar rows, the way `reservedValueHint` marks atlas's reserved values. The zone page: "Synced" replaces the v1 "Local only" lie, an inherits line under the colour ("Colour from the world style for Line · River" / "Use the kind's colour"), point tables past 8 fold behind the map row (+ pan-to-zone), and the `crosshair` glyph (never in the font, rendered as the word) is `my_location`. Sikelia styled in five clicks: rivers blue w3 label-first, routes grey w1 dashed, chorai amber (`23-map-styled.png`). 8 unit tests; e2e in `phase5-maps` (kinds, rule → strokes + disk, hover, eye, search, reload, zone page). `name-sort-guarded` caught an unguarded `.name.localeCompare` in the new panel: the conformance law (only `name` is required, and even that may be absent) has teeth.
- **BOUT THREE (Captain's smoke of the panel, same day)**: **the colour popover** (`ui/colour-popover.ts`: 14 presets commit on click, hex + Apply/Enter, Cancel/Escape/outside dismiss; the native dialog committed every drag, could not be styled and painted a white box under the swatch; the fold-out's outside-click ignores it). **Toolbar**: fit-to-image moved right, beside open-map-element (a view-state control); left = settings | show/hide | draw. **Muted types** (`core/type-mutes.ts`, `ui.mutedTypes`, per world in localStorage): the sidebar's third filter state, out of "show all" and in when filtered to; pin + marker muted by default and always the strip's last two slots (they are IN the strip now, so 196 pins / 3,143 markers are browsable on request); right-click toggles, greyed + struck. This replaced the per-type right-click "refresh from API" (a global pull dressed as a type action; the sync pill's pull-now is its home). Own eyes: `30-colour-popover.png`, `32-sidebar-muted.png`. e2e: the panel test drives the new picker; a mutes test in `phase4-elements`. Changelog 1.5 is nine entries.
- **PERF, the safe part (Captain: zoom is laggy)**: CDP profile of six zoom steps on Sikelia: JS is a rounding error (density 12ms, kernel 2ms); the cost is `getBoundingClientRect` forcing layout on a tree Leaflet just dirtied (288ms / 6 zooms) and `(program)` = the browser's own style/layout/paint of ~1,000 marker DIVs + 625 SVG paths (1.3s / 6). Done: the density pass now runs after the next paint (`scheduleLabelDensity`, rAF + timeout, coalesced), halving the forced layout; the map subscriber no longer re-syncs 300 zones on every ui tick (the eye calls `refreshActiveMapZones` directly); the zone panel skips its innerHTML rebuild while closed and renders on open (`atlas:zone-panel-open`). Zoom steps 70-120ms → still DOM-bound. **The real fix is a canvas renderer for pins + labels (a session, not today); banked.**

## PRIOR (2026-09-04 — SESSION 355: **FIND A WORD, LINK EVERY ONE OF THEM** — a Discord request reframed and shipped at both tiers; gates: typecheck 0 + **1609 vitest** + writing-workspace e2e 37/37 (full e2e not re-run this session; S354's 295 stands for the rest)).

**Full record: `Notes/2026-09-04-s355-find-link.md`.** The request (mika, Discord, 2026-09-03): "search narrative text, kinda like a ctrl f, for keywords and be able to link all of them to a specific element". What existed: select-and-"Link to existing element" (one occurrence, teaches the alias), link-all (every element, FIRST mention only), a find/replace bar (text only, scenes only: parents open in the compiled view, which had no editor and therefore no find at all).

- **THE FIND BAR LINKS** (`writing-workspace-find-bar.ts`, `data-role="link-matches"`): a "Link to element…" button beside the toggles, enabled when there are matches. Picker, then every match in the scene becomes a chip in ONE transaction (`SearchController.linkAllMatches`, Ctrl+Z undoes), labels verbatim (convention #49b), the word recorded as an alias when foreign (`recordAliasIfForeign`, now exported from `prose-link-existing.ts`). This is deliberately NOT first-mention: the writer named the word and picked the target (convention #49e).
- **FIND IN THE COMPILED VIEW** (`writing-compiled-search.ts`, new): a DOM-marking `SearchController` over the read-only rendering (`<mark class="atlas-search-match">`, current + scrollIntoView, never enters a chip or a section marker). The workspace's find proxy now routes to the editor OR this, whichever is mounted. Ctrl+H there opens FIND with `data-readonly="true"` (replace is refused: the rendering is not the source). "Link to element" runs `bulkLinkTerm` over root + `collectNarrativeDescendants` (both story and sidecar on disk, relation derive on each, the live narrative through `linkTermInEditor` if one is open), then **remounts the compiled view** and re-applies the highlight. Toast names count + narratives + "written to disk, no undo".
- **Kernel** (`core/prose-autolink.ts`): `buildTermScanner` (now THE scanner: the search plugin imports it, so what the bar highlights is exactly what links), `linkTermMarkdown`, `linkTermRichtext`. 9 new unit tests incl. an md/rt count-agreement sweep over all four flag combinations.
- **FIXED ON THE WAY**: the find bar was orphaned by `root.innerHTML = ''` on narrative switch, so Ctrl+F after moving between scenes opened a detached, invisible bar (`ensureFindBar` rebuilds when detached; e2e locks it). And `mountCompiledView` REPLACES the host's class, so anything looking the host up by the prose class afterwards finds nothing (my first remount did exactly that; the e2e's stale-view screenshot caught it).
- **Changelog 1.4** (three entries, two credited to mika/discord) regenerated.
- **CAPTAIN'S SMOKE, three fixes the same session**: (1) a padded term ("orc ") was becoming the chip label → `trimMatchSpan` shrinks every match to its non-whitespace core in all four link paths (md, rt, live linkAllMatches, live linkTerm); (2) the linked word turned into the element NAME in two ways, both closed: the compiled renderer printed `el?.name ?? label` (RULED: never; `renderInline` now emits the label, unit test flipped, the share preview inherits it, `tools/show` copy re-lift owed), and the chip-label sync read a padded label as stale (`isValidChipLabel` now trims; alias is recorded BEFORE chips land so the sync tick cannot race it); (3) Ctrl+F / Ctrl+H seed the box from the selection (editor selection in this-level, `window.getSelection()` in compiled). Still pre-existing: the find bar floats over the crumb row's date pill at `top: 44px`.
- **The trap that fired twice this session, for the record**: bash heredocs ate a `\b` in the kernel (a backspace character inside a template literal, found by the whole-word unit test) and truncated an e2e append mid-line. Scripts to files; appends byte-exact from a file. And three working-copy files (`atlas.css`, the kernel test, this file) were CRLF against an LF HEAD; normalised.
- **BOUT THREE (autonomous, Captain's "go full autonomous at it all")**: **UNLINKED MENTIONS** (`core/unlinked-mentions.ts`, pure, cached per elements-array reference): the element preview in the context pane gets a line beside "Linked from", *"Mentioned, unlinked (8 in 5 scenes): Scene A (3), Scene C (2), … · Link all"* — the standing home for mika's first sentence ("make a new element, link every matching word"), present on the page you land on after creating and on every existing element. Link all runs `bulkLinkTerm` over name ∪ aliases (whole-word, case-insensitive, `MENTION_MATCH`) across every listed narrative and the card rebuilds; the count and the link share ONE span finder (`findTermSpansMarkdown`) so the number shown is the number written (unit-tested as an identity). The create modal's success toast carries the nudge ("· appears in 2 narratives, unlinked"). `buildTermScanner` takes term arrays now (longest first). **Tidies**: `tools/show` render.ts re-lifted to the label rule (its own repo, uncommitted there) · find bar sits below the crumb row (`top: 76px`) · the compiled view re-renders on every elements-array swap, not only on narrative switch · the search plugin's dead `decorationsFor` placeholder deleted · the compiled search's mark order was reversed across nodes ("1 of 9" lit the last scene's word; own-eyes caught it, e2e locks document order). Gates: typecheck 0 (atlas + show) · **1620 vitest** (+8) · writing + context e2e 60/60 · full e2e at close (below). Shots: `Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-09-04-s355/`.
- **BOUT FOUR (the tidy before quench; Skeld's v0.3.6 letter arrived mid-session)**: three folder-format rulings built the same day. **§4.1**: `linkExistingApiWorldInRoot` writes the SERVER id as `id` (`linkedWorldMeta`, pure, unit-tested for `id === api.world_id`); vault key `world-<id>` with a numeric suffix if linked twice; earlier links keep their minted ids. **§4.2**: `format_version: "0.3.6"` stamped by `FsFolderStore.writeWorldMeta` ONLY when no `world.json` existed (birth), mirrored in `MockFolderStore`; a foreign folder edited in atlas is never given one (e2e in `folder-store-fs.spec.ts`). `snapshot_counts` and `.gitignore`: atlas never wrote either (grep, not memory). Two readings sent back to Skeld for a possible overrule: birth-only stamping, and Route 1 keeping its minted id (the folder predates the server object). Also: `tools/show` README notes the re-lift, `DESIGN_WRITING` v3.3, the atlas-tool skill carries the extended label law, `tools.json` S355 line, Boss briefed with a three-probe smoke (`Hangar/.boss/messages/inbox/from-temper-2026-09-04-…`). ## PRIOR (2026-07-28 — SESSION 334: **THE UNION READ** — atlas can finally open a world written by anyone else; gates: typecheck 0 + **1595 vitest** + **293 e2e (count = `--list` = 293, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0, zero flaky)**; UNCOMMITTED, and carrying the S331 type-stamp fix + the four S320 files + the whole S323 pile)

**The bug, stated once**: `elements/<type>/` is the Folder Format for all 22 types (spec v0.3). Atlas was the only implementation that split maps, pins, zones and markers into `spatial/<type>/`, and it read ONLY there. So a world written by any other tool — Kael's snapshot writer, magelet, a third party — opened showing every content element, a clean UI, and **no maps, pins, zones or markers at all, with no warning anywhere**. Kael has two verified captures (36 and 1,087 elements) and a live Blood Meridian world carrying 1 map + 1 zone that shipped atlas could not show.

- **THE UNION READ.** `buildIndexFor` now scans BOTH layouts for spatial types and merges; every element's `fileIndex` entry records the `layout` it was actually found in, and every write, rename, delete and sync-stamp goes back to that same directory. New worlds write `elements/` for all 22 and **never create `spatial/`**; a folder that already holds spatial files keeps receiving them there (detected at open by FILE presence — an empty `spatial/map/` is litter from the old `ensureLayout`, not a layout). `elementDirSegments(type)` is GONE, replaced by `elementDirSegmentsIn(layout, type)`: the layout was an invisible property of the TYPE and belongs to the FOLDER, and no caller gets to omit it now.
- **Cross-layout twins are not crash twins.** Same id in both directories: newest `local_updated_at` serves, ties go to `elements/`, a warning names both paths, and **neither file is touched**. Same-directory duplicates are still deduped (a rename crash); resolving someone else's folder by deleting half of it is not atlas's call. The tidy runs per (type, layout) — two directories, two occupancy spaces — and a shadowed file, being absent from the index, is never renamed.
- **THE CONFORMANCE FIXTURE** (`tests/fixtures/folder-conformance/`) — Kael's proposal, adopted by every pass of Skeld's review round, built portable for the public sibling repo it is destined for. One tiny world + `expected.json` + a README stating the contract; `tests/e2e/folder-conformance.spec.ts` is atlas answering it. It covers the whole family in one artifact: only `name` required, body type beats directory, id-less file skipped AND left on disk, unknown `x_*` round-trips, a **mapless pin**, and the legacy/normative split (a reader covering only `elements/` is conforming; atlas covers both tiers). **Counts live in `expected.json`'s arrays and nowhere else** — this line said "6 and 7" until 2026-07-31, true when written and stale from the moment S334 grew the answer key.
- **Both halves revert-checked**: read reverted → 2 loud failures; write reverted → 2 loud failures.
- **OWN EYES, through the real open-folder path** (`Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-07-28-union-read/`): the fixture world opens with all 7 elements, both maps in the map index, the map canvas mounting clean with a mapless pin in the world, one honest warning (the id-less file, now naming its layout), **zero console errors** — and the folder afterwards byte-identical, every file at its original path.
- **SKELD'S TWO FLAGS, answered by source read** (method stated, not a live probe): **mapless pins** degrade correctly — the pin/marker header narrows `map_id` to `string | undefined` and renders no affordance when absent, and every map filter compares `map_id === mapId` so an absent one simply never matches. **`<img src="">`** does not reproduce — all 18 `img.src`/`src=` sites are guarded by truthiness or an `^https?://` test, the payload builder only emits `image_url` when non-empty, and the four off-DOM `new Image()` helpers take their url from `resolveMapImageSource`, which requires non-empty. One honest divergence: absent `marker.order` is tolerated everywhere (`?? 0`) but atlas does **not** fall back to `created_at`, so a fully order-less zone draws in file-scan order.
- **★ CLOSED THE FORMAT'S BIGGEST EVIDENCE GAP, AND IT FOUND A SECOND UNRULED RENAME.** Skeld's ask was that Kael's writer had never been opened in a live reader. I ran his built CLI against demo world 0 and opened the capture verbatim — **not my fixture**, because a fixture carries my assumptions and none of the producer's. **36 of 36 elements**, spatial types included, charts rendering his dated events with a live calendar lens (so `atlas_calendar`, a nested object, round-trips). **But his `world.json` carries `time_range_current` (the wire name, per spec) while atlas's disk name is `time_current`** — so a captured world silently loses its NOW: no now-line, and a "set now time" button offering to set a value the folder already holds. Same class as §5's bare-link-fields ruling I made and never built: **atlas holds disk↔wire renames the format never adjudicated, and each is a silent loss the day a second implementation appears.** Two now, both mine. NOT fixed tonight on purpose (a disk contract spanning spec + writer + reader is not mine to rule at midnight); recommendation to Skeld is that the WIRE name is canonical on disk and atlas reads both forever.
- **★ RULED AND SHIPPED THE SAME NIGHT, AND IT INVERTED ME.** I recommended the WIRE name as canonical on disk, arguing against my own bay. Skeld and Kael independently checked the **canonical schema** and overturned it: `schema/world.yaml:59` says **`time_current`** — atlas's disk name IS the standard's name, and keel is the deviation (`rehearsal/transform.py:249`, `# RENAME (R5)`, a v1→v2 migration that never updated the standard). **My error: I had three documents available and compared two**, reasoning from a sound principle onto a premise I never checked. Familiarity with my own code read as verification. Shipped in twelve minutes: `readWorldMeta` fills `time_current` from `time_range_current` when absent, **fill-only, never rewriting** — spec v0.3.5 rules that a reader MUST NOT migrate a key spelling as a side effect of opening a folder, and `writeWorldMeta` is a read-merge-write of the whole object. Revert-checked (42 → null). **My sticky-write rule is now spec** as the general rule for any name the format has two spellings of. Kael's writer takes the one line; atlas needed nothing else.
- **★ THE STORAGE-BOUNDARY CENSUS (Skeld's ask): no third rename, but a GENERATOR.** `editor/field-schema.ts:WIRE_SHAPE_TYPES` does not rename two known fields — it suffixes **every** link field on pin and marker, present and future. The day the Council adds one, atlas invents a private `_id` disk name nobody decided. **§5's debt is an open tap that fires on schema growth, not a two-field tidy-up**; priority raised, comment added at the site. World meta holds exactly one hardcoded rename (resolved above). Everything else at every storage boundary is passthrough, and the four element-field consumers all derive from one authority.
- **The fixture grew four malformed cases on Quillon's ask** (unparseable file, empty-string name, explicit nulls, plus the id-less file) — he argues the malformed cases are worth more than the well-formed ones and he is right. **One caught me**: I named the empty-name file non-canonically, atlas's tidy correctly renamed it, and my own byte-identical test went red. **Kael ran the fixture with an independent reader** sharing no code with atlas: 6/6 elements-only, 7 with `--legacy`, zero bytes mutated. Two implementations, one answer key.
- ⚡ **SUPERSEDED — the `time_current` bout is DONE** (kept for the reasoning): Scoped honestly after Captain asked when it would actually happen: **atlas must read BOTH spellings under either ruling** (its own worlds carry `time_current` forever; captures carry `time_range_current`), and the write side is answered by the union read's own pattern — read both, write back whatever the folder already uses, default only for something new. The hazard that makes it a bout rather than a rename: `writeWorldMeta` is a read-merge-write of the whole meta object, so a naive normalize-on-read would silently rewrite a key **inside a frozen capture** on the next meta write. **TRIGGER: the opening bout of the next atlas session, ruling or no ruling.** With Skeld's answer it also sets the new-world default; without it, ship the read-and-sticky-write half (correct either way) and leave the one constant behind a comment naming the open question. Ask sent 2026-07-28, deliberately shrunk to that single default.
- ⚡ **ALSO NEXT**: Captain's smoke (list in the session note) · the commit, which now carries S320 + S323 + S331 + S334 · **`tools/show` re-lift is NOT affected** (payload-side, no folder reads) · then the S324 opener as written (map-canvas token census, waypoint toast, the charts-island ruling) · banked unchanged: `revokeIfPublished` · icon-font subsetting · og:image · World-green trade.

## PRIOR (2026-07-26 — SESSION 323: **THE FIRST POST-LAUNCH SWEEP** — a 12-agent refute-verified critical sweep run BEHIND a keeper own-eyes walk, then seven bouts of fixes; gates: typecheck 0 + **1595 vitest** + **285 e2e (count = `--list` = 285, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0)** + build clean; UNCOMMITTED, and carrying the four S320 files that were never committed)

**Full record: `Notes/2026-07-26-s323-sweep-findings.md`** (the findings sheet, written as a work order, with the verdicts and the corrections to my own claims).

The instrument split held: six read-only lanes (responsive / cold-stranger / weight / light-mode / docs-drift / data-safety), each refute-verified by an opus skeptic, 41 findings and 41 verdicts (18 CONFIRMED, 22 NARROWED, 1 REFUTED), while I drove the live app at 1440 / 1024 / 820 / 640 / 390 in both themes. The fleet found the source; the walk found the surface; neither found the other's.

- **PROSE LOSS IN THE WRITING ROOM, closed.** `persistSnapshot` resolved its write target from the LIVE url and bailed when that disagreed with the mounted editor, after `flushPendingSave` had already cleared the pending snapshot — so text typed inside the debounce window and then abandoned (leaving the room, or switching narrative) was gone from editor, state and disk, silently, in a tool with no undo. The write is now addressed by the id captured at `scheduleSave` time; the route is no longer consulted. Two e2e, revert-checked (2 loud failures). **My first narrowing was WRONG and the revert-check caught it**: I had reported the tree-switch route safe on the strength of a hand-driven repro, but each Playwright MCP round trip costs a second or two and I burned the whole 6s debounce window locating the row before clicking it. A laggy instrument cannot establish a negative about a timing window.
- **THE SHARE PREVIEW SHOWED THE WRONG ARTIFACT.** `[data-mode='dark']` had no CSS at all — dark was inherited free from a shell that was always dark, and light mode falsified the premise, so a dark page previewed light while `show` rendered it correctly. Dark is pinned now, as a shared DARK-ISLAND re-pin also used by the share pin modal. Triplets alone were not enough (the light block defines `--atlas-text*` as flat literals); measured, not assumed. Guard asserts luminance in BOTH themes, revert-checked.
- **LIGHT MODE, three real surfaces.** Map pin labels and tooltips were white-on-white (themed scrim + literal `#fff`) — new `--atlas-canvas-scrim-rgb`/`--atlas-canvas-ink-rgb` pair for chrome that floats over the user's artwork and must not flip. 26 literal `color: #fff` swept to `rgb(var(--atlas-ink-rgb))`, which is white inside every pinned dark island and correct outside it. The knowledge and share rooms, the two never own-eyed in light and the flagged risk, were **clean**.
- **THE NARROW-WINDOW LAYER (`src/layout/narrow-layout.ts`).** The shell had a ~706px floor of pane min-widths and THREE media queries in ~14,800 lines, none touching it, inside containers that hide overflow everywhere: panes did not shrink, they left the screen unreachably. Below 1100 the right pane becomes a drawer, below 820 the sidebar joins it, with a scrim, Escape, hashchange dismissal and one-drawer-at-a-time. **The phone tour collapse was a symptom of this plus one more thing**: the dock is fixed-height for desktop no-jitter, so at 390 the sentence's words overflowed their reserved box and painted over the copy. Own-eyes at every width; desktop dock measured byte-identical (290px). New `narrow-layout.spec.ts` is the suite's first width-aware spec — and its first draft PASSED against the broken CSS, because it compared BOXES while the overlap was rendered INK. Fixed to measure the words, then it failed by 106px.
- **SAFETY**: a transient `NotFoundError` during world open deleted the registry entry AND the saved handle on a name-matched guess; it now asks the filesystem first. A folder I/O throw in the sync meta step pinned the pill at "Saving…" for the session (the third instance of the S308 one-sided-hardening class; the code named the hole itself and deferred it). A failed dependents probe silently disabled the S310 cascade confirm floor. Revoked keys got PIN advice.
- **COPY**: the landing told phone visitors on Chrome that they needed Chrome, and offered them no forward door; both fixed (the example page is now reachable from the landing). The demo page advertised links its payload does not contain. The tour finale pointed at a footer the landing state hides. Two settings-tab names, an em-dash sweep with a source-scan guard, and a phantom changelog toolbar removed rather than built.
- **WEIGHT**: `owlogo.png` was 4748×4748 (426KB) to draw a 15px icon, now 512 (68KB, the size its own PWA manifest declares); `og.png` left the precache. SW precache 6490 → 5899 KiB.
- **DOCS**: three live docs stated a RETIRED v1 rule as current (all `atlas_*` stripped on write) when v2 splits local-only from wire extensions and zone visuals genuinely sync. Corrected in place with dated notes rather than quiet edits.
- **CAPTAIN'S FIRST LOOK (same evening, one screenshot of a real world in light mode) found the half S323 did not do: the surfaces that are READABLE but disagree with each other.** Full opener: `Notes/2026-07-26-s324-opener.md`. (1) **Zone labels and pin labels are now opposite on the same map** — pin labels went to the canvas tokens in S323 (dark chip, white text), zone labels still ride the app scrim (white chip, dark text). That is convention #90 half-applied ON THE DAY I WROTE IT; the fix is two declarations, the work is the census of everything else drawing over the map canvas. (2) **The waypoint toast is a bare dark literal** (`atlas.css:14082`) sitting beside a themed toast in the same corner. (3+4) **The charts room and the home chart preview are still black** — that is the S315 dark island working as designed, not drift, and changing it is measured at ~56 JS colour literals across `timeline-view.ts` + `chart-static.ts` plus the element-colour light column: a session, not an afternoon. A cheaper middle option (light-mode FRAME around a still-dark plot) is in the opener.
- ⚡ **NEXT**: Captain's smoke (checklist in the findings sheet) · the commit + push, which also carries the four S320 files · then S324 as laid out in the opener: the map-canvas token census, the toast call, and Captain's ruling on the charts island before anything is built there · still banked from before: `revokeIfPublished` · icon-font subsetting, the highest-leverage weight fix (ask Kael first, it may want to be an Assembly card) · og:image pick · World-green trade · `tools/show` redeploy.

## PRIOR (2026-07-23 — SESSION 313: **THE NOTES BOUT** — a long batch of Captain's field-report fixes across the whole tool; gates: typecheck 0 + **1505 vitest** + **270 e2e, PLAYWRIGHT_EXIT=0** + build clean; committed `4efc973`)

A wide sweep of Captain's live-use notes, each own-eyes- or test-verified. Grouped:

- **IMPORT — reads the keel envelope now, and round-trips.** ow.com's export changed under the keel rebuild to the versioned `onlyworlds-world-export` v0.30.00 envelope (lowercase `world` WITH id, nested `elements{}` by lowercase type). Atlas only knew the old `World`+PascalCase shape, so Captain's Hyperion file 0-matched. `parse.ts` now detects+unwraps the envelope (format tag + structural fallback) AND still reads the old `World`/loose shapes (backward-compat). **Adopts the exported world id** (`scaffoldWorldInRoot(adoptId)` — lossless round-trip; Skeld's id-is-identity law) when no local world has it, else offers **merge-into-it OR import-as-separate-copy** (idMatch by `world-<id>` handle key; merge activates the target then reconciles against ITS elements). `change_seq` added to the validator's server-stamp skip (was 36 bogus warnings). Real file verified end-to-end: 36 clean creates, 0 warnings. Skeld ruling: envelope is canonical (keel `portal/views.py`), he's freezing a `§ world-export` spec section; atlas's EXPORT converges onto the envelope post-launch (Q2, owed).
- **AUTOLINK (writing + knowledge + share, shared plugin) — reliable now.** Click a dotted word to link it (works unfocused); Tab is HOVER-AUTHORITATIVE (hovering a word + Tab links THAT, not the first); right-click dismisses the chip-preview so it never obscures the Unlink menu; chip-preview open delay 220→480ms. - **SPATIAL affordances (`element-detail-header.ts`).** A pin/marker's own detail shows a clickable "open its map" button (opens + pans); `+pin` hidden once the element already has a pin on the ACTIVE map, and hidden entirely for pin/marker/zone types (not pinnable).

- **ZONES — labels below by default.** New `zone_label_position` pref (default `'below'`): the label hangs off the zone's bottom-centre edge instead of the centroid (was blocked by fill/markers). Control in the Maps settings tab + each map's ⚙ popover. `ZoneOverlay` `labelAnchorFor`/`bottomCenter`.

- **TOUR create-preview stack (`buildCreateStack`).** Now previews everything that ARRIVES by clicking Next — scripted `creates` ∪ `reveal` lists (so deep-time's Rachmaninov/Prelude + the-rest's ability/trait/… finally get icons), NOT the hand-made consul/pin/link. Floated OUT of flow (was a vertical column that pushed Back/Exit down): a right-anchored VERTICAL column, capped at 4 + a "+N" chip for busy reveal steps (the map beat = map+zone+6 markers → 4 icons + "+4"). Own-eyes verified (`Notes/ui-snapshots/2026-07-23-s313/`).
- **TOUR grey timing + set-now chip.** Sentence greys from step 3 (was 4). The "set now time" button: was a barely-visible cyan pulse fighting the warm-gold "now" it sets → now a FILLED warm-gold highlight during the beat + a subtle warm base fill so it's spottable generally.
